# Data Plan

## 2026-10-21 — BISCUIT becomes PIX.MOV's single consent gate

**User direction**: Incorporate BISCUIT as PIX.MOV's cookie/consent launcher — the single consent gate for the whole app. On first visit BISCUIT asks permission to record data for PIX.MOV use, with clear Accept and Decline choices and links to a Privacy Policy page and a consent Agreements page (both must exist with real content drawn from the app's existing privacy policy and `~/workspace/pix-network/LEGAL.md`). The recorded decision gates data recording: declining means no non-essential recording (no personalization / FILM DNA / ad-measurement capture beyond what the page needs to function). Unify consent into ONE truth by bridging BISCUIT to the existing profile fields (`cookie_decision` / `personalization` / `ad_measurement` via `savePixConsent`) so decisions already recorded carry over. A persistent "Cookie & Data Consent" link (footer and profile) lets users change their decision later. The existing signup privacy accept/decline flow keeps working inside this single system, not as a parallel one.

**Implementation**: The game's existing consent panel is rebranded and lifted as `BiscuitLauncher`, mounted once at App level by `BiscuitConsentGate`: it presents whenever the saved decision is `pending` (except on the two legal pages, so they can be read unobstructed), and re-opens on the `pix:open-consent` window event — dispatched by the game's consent blocks (the game's local panel state was removed), the footer and profile "Cookie & Data Consent" links, and the legal pages' status boxes. The launcher writes through the existing `savePixConsent` action, so previously recorded decisions carry over untouched. Two new pages: `#privacy` (Privacy Policy) and `#agreements` (Consent Agreements), with content drawn from the app's existing in-app privacy policy text (live since 2026-09-30) and the data inventory, standing rules, and known gaps in PIX.MOV Legal & Compliance v1.0.0 (2026-10-21) — including the PIX privacy rule (voluntary demographics only for games + choosing entertainment), aggregate levels (Global / Country / State-Province), the verified-email rule for CINE, the 70/30 creator share and locked pricing, the honest gaps (outbound email not connected — codes shown as labeled in-app previews; Google Ads not live), and each page shows the viewer's live BISCUIT decision. Server-side, one pair of helpers — `pixRecordingAllowed` (decision accepted AND personalization on) and `pixConsentPending` — now guards every personal-recording action: `recordPixVote`, `recordPixPairSkip`, `markPixUnseen`, `recordPixActorVote`, `recordPixInterest`, `markPixSeen`, `triagePixCatalogBatch`, `dismissPixMarqueeMovie`, and the FILM DNA synopsis search's preference context + query logging. Previously these keyed only on `personalization`, whose schema default is true, so a not-yet-decided (pending) viewer could be recorded through non-game paths; pending now records nothing and returns `requires_consent: true`, declined records nothing with `requires_consent: false` (a decision already made), and accepted + personalization records exactly as before. The profile window's recording toggles were also unified: they refine only an accepted decision (the save now preserves the current decision instead of forcing "accepted"), and flipping one while declined/pending routes to BISCUIT instead of writing consent. The signup flow is unchanged in mechanics — its privacy checkbox and startSignup still write through `savePixConsent` — and its policy details now link the two legal pages. No migration: the consent fields already exist on `pix_profiles`.

## 2026-10-21 — Customizable landing page rows (fold + drag reorder, per-user)

**User direction**: Make the PIX.MOV landing page customizable like the CINE workspace — every genre/channel row on the landing page (Horror, Comedy, Recently Uploaded, and all other rows) gets a `>` fold control in its header that folds the row's posters down/up; users can drag rows up and down by their headers to reorder them; the user's CUSTOM LISTS shown on the landing page are draggable the same way; the arrangement (row order and folded state) is remembered per user, following the app's existing per-user preference patterns.

**Implementation**: New `landing_layout` TEXT column on `pix_profiles` (migration `0067_add_landing_layout_to_pix_profile.sql`) storing JSON `{order, collapsed}`, read/written by `getPixLandingLayout` / `savePixLandingLayout` — the same profile-backed pattern as `attractions_row_order` (0063) and `cine_bin_dock` (0064). Built-in row keys: `top_rated`, `horror`, `recent`, `comedy`, `user_content`, `trending`, `hidden_gems`; custom-list rows ride as `list:<id>` keys and are rendered on the landing page from the existing `listPixUserLists` action (poster-backed films only, per the standing poster rule). Client: every landing row header (`PixHomeRowHeader`) carries a ⠿ grip (arms header dragging, the ATTRACTIONS pattern), ↑/↓ move buttons (touch/keyboard path for the same reorder), and a `>` fold chevron (`aria-expanded`); drag slots highlight with a gold outline and shaded fill while a row hovers them. No external data sources; no new rows seeded.

**Refinements (same day, Brian's follow-up)**: (1) Row controls moved to the row's TAG LINE — the title stands on its own line, and the tag line (e.g. Horror's "Terror from the public-domain vault") carries the note on the left with the grip/↑/↓/fold controls right-aligned at the line's right end. (2) The ATTRACTIONS bar gained a gold GENRES button (black text; the bar's other buttons — now including PIX.MOV LISTS — are black with gold outline, label kept small). GENRES opens a drop-down of every genre with a landing row; selecting one unfolds that row if folded and scrolls to it (a `pix:open-landing-row` window event handled by `PixHomeSections`). To give every genre a row, `listPixClassicSections` now also emits one `genre:<slug>` section per genre label (via `pixGenreLabels`) holding at least two vault films — from the current vault: Action, Adventure, Drama, Family, Fantasy, Mystery, Romance, Sci-Fi, Thriller, War (single-film labels — Crime, Documentary, History, Music, Western, Short — stay row-less until a second vault film joins them; Häxan remains reachable in the Horror row). Landing-layout validation accepts `genre:<slug>` keys alongside built-ins and `list:<id>`.

## 2026-10-21 — Four silent dramas wired (Greed, Sunrise, The Crowd, The Big Parade) + film stills

**User direction**: Wire 4 newly verified-live public-domain films into the app with the same INGEST §8 pattern as the previous 31 — Greed (1924, 120 clips), Sunrise (1927, 48 clips), The Crowd (1928, 47 clips), The Big Parade (1925, 77 clips), all Drama — each with a SCREENING (FEATURE PRESENTATION) page, a CINE PIX LIBRARY entry with working ADD to BIN, and Recently Uploaded channel placement. NEW: these four carry a `still_url` in `pix_classic_films` (a still frame hosted on B2); use the still for the PIX LIBRARY card and the movie preview display wherever a `still_url` exists, falling back to the poster when it doesn't.

**Source of record**: the ingest pipeline's per-film manifests at `~/workspace/pix-network/classic-clips/{greed-1924,sunrise-1927,the-crowd-1928,the-big-parade-1925}/manifest.json` (INGEST v1.2.0). Each manifest records the B2 prefix under `public-domain/drama/<year>/<filmKey>/clips/`, the verified clip list (`b2_url_verified: true`, `upload_status: uploaded`), and the `still_url`. The pipeline registered the film and clip rows directly in `pix_classic_films` / `pix_classic_clips` (including an external `ALTER TABLE` adding `still_url`); read-only verification against `app.db` confirmed all four films, 292 clips with non-empty `stream_url`, and the four still URLs below. Catalog matches in `pix_movie_stats` supply poster, genre (Drama-led), synopsis, and IMDb id for each film (Greed tt0015881, Sunrise tt0018455, The Crowd tt0018806, The Big Parade tt0015624), and all four poster files are bundled client assets.

**Still URLs (verbatim from the manifests)**:
- Greed — `https://s3.us-east-005.backblazeb2.com/PIXMOV/public-domain/drama/1924/greed-1924/still.jpg`
- Sunrise — `https://s3.us-east-005.backblazeb2.com/PIXMOV/public-domain/drama/1927/sunrise-1927/still.jpg`
- The Crowd — `https://s3.us-east-005.backblazeb2.com/PIXMOV/public-domain/drama/1928/the-crowd-1928/still.jpg`
- The Big Parade — `https://s3.us-east-005.backblazeb2.com/PIXMOV/public-domain/drama/1925/the-big-parade-1925/still.jpg`

**Wiring**: the `still_url` column itself was added to the live database by the ingest pipeline's own `ALTER TABLE` (a duplicate `ADD COLUMN` in a migration fails against it, verified at build time), so `server/src/schema.ts` declares the column and migration `0066_add_still_url_and_wire_four_drama_classics.sql` upserts the four film rows with their still URLs; `PIX_CLASSIC_FILMS` in `server/src/actions.ts` carries the four definitions (with `stillUrl`), Drama genre fallbacks, and the Recently Uploaded pin leads with the four. `pixClassicFilmResponse` and the section card now expose `still_url`. Client: the CINE PIX LIBRARY film card shows the still (poster fallback), library clip rows prefer per-clip grab → film still → poster, the FEATURE PRESENTATION player uses the still as its poster frame, and its clip tiles prefer the per-clip grab, then the film still, instead of loading a video element per tile. Landing/channel cards remain poster-based per the standing poster rule. `ensurePixClassicCollection` syncs a definition's still but never clears a stored still, so the pipeline's backfilled stills for the earlier films survive reads.

## 2026-10-19 — ATTRACTIONS, with COMB supplying Row 2 (Biggest Financial Success)

**User direction**: Create "ATTRACTIONS" (taxonomy term) with ATTRACTION ROW 1 (Top 500 films of all time), ROW 2 (Biggest financial success), ROW 3 (a game that helps you discover what movies you've seen this year or from your past), and ROW 4 — source materials + games + lists + reviews for why one would visit PIX.MOV. COMB discovered and reported top grossing films; wire COMB's findings to supply the Biggest financial success row.

**How COMB supplies PIX**: COMB is the sibling static artifact at `~/workspace/ts-spaces/comb/`; its published page (`index.html`) embeds its reported chart as a `TOP100` data array of `[title, year, worldwide gross US$]` — the artifact's own findings, not a live feed. The wiring is a one-way supply line: the 100 rows were extracted verbatim from COMB's published array (programmatically, no retyping) into PIX migration `0058_add_comb_grossers_attractions.sql`, stored in the `pix_comb_grossers` table with each row's source label, and read by the `listPixCombGrossers` action, which powers both ATTRACTION ROW 2 and the Biggest Financial Success page (`#grossers`). If COMB's chart is ever updated, re-running the same extraction refreshes the table.

**Provenance (as COMB itself reports it)**: ranks 1–50 from Wikipedia, "List of highest-grossing films"; ranks 51–100 continue in gross order from Box Office Mojo's all-time worldwide chart; checked Oct 2026. COMB's rank order is preserved exactly as reported, including its one near-tie ordering (Harry Potter and the Deathly Hallows – Part 1 at rank 51). Grosses are worldwide totals in US$; PIX does not adjust or estimate them.

**Poster resolution**: COMB's chart carries titles and years only. `listPixCombGrossers` matches each entry to the PIX catalog (`pix_movie_stats`) by normalized title + release year (one alias: Philosopher's Stone ↔ Sorcerer's Stone), so matched films show their real owned poster and link to their screening page. Unmatched films are returned with `in_catalog: false` and render as honest title tiles — no poster is ever borrowed from another film, and no figure is dropped from the chart.

**The four rows**: Row 1 previews `listPixTopMovies` and opens `#top500`; Row 2 previews COMB's chart and opens `#grossers`; Row 3 is the Sort Collection discovery game (`#sort`) with live per-user sort stats from `getPixSortStats` and a current-year (2026) poster preview from `listPixBrowseCatalog`; Row 4 previews the community's latest reviews from `listPixFriendReviews` and opens the SHOWCASE (`#profile`), with an honest empty state before any review exists.

# Data Plan

## Context provenance
- `JUST MAKE A COPY of BRAINSTORM and CALL it PIX` (verbatim request; establishes that PIX should duplicate Brainstorm’s current source, capability set, visual system, owned media, and server-backed schema rather than become a new standalone movie game).
- `WAIT!!!! I WANTED two off the same instances but one called PIX and the other called BRAINSTORM - BRAINSTORM has CIRCLE shapes in header - PIX has square. CLEAR?` (parent-conversation clarification; establishes separate artifacts and the PIX-specific square-header distinction).
- `/home/hatch/workspace/ts-spaces/brainstorm/` (builder-inspected source artifact; current source includes Notes as the foundational data layer, Dream Log, Posters, Monsters, PIX/Performance games, CINE, Distributor, Timeline, Library, Profile, chat, backup, deploy staging, and all owned media).
- `/home/hatch/workspace/ts-spaces/brainstorm/DATA-PLAN.md` (existing source provenance; preserved with the clone so the owned images, poster sources, Wikidata actor roster, CINE music loops, and publishing guidance retain their established provenance).

## Tested sources
### Existing Brainstorm source artifact
**Used by**: the complete PIX clone — client, typed server actions, schema, migrations, bundled assets, and existing capability wiring.
**Test command**: `read /home/hatch/workspace/ts-spaces/brainstorm/{space.json,package.json,client/src,server/src,drizzle,DATA-PLAN.md}`
**Sample output**: Brainstorm is a platformVersion 22 TypeScript artifact with React client files (`App.tsx`, `PixSection.tsx`, `PixProfileSection.tsx`), server actions, schema, eleven migrations, owned poster/concept assets, and the current dark archive interface.
**Processing**: Copy source and owned assets into `pix-2`; retain target metadata name `PIX` and slug `pix-2`; do not copy build products, audits, node_modules, runtime icon files, or Brainstorm’s live database files. Fresh PIX storage is created from the cloned migrations, keeping the two artifacts independent.

### Owned visual assets from Brainstorm
**Used by**: Posters, Monsters, NOW PLAYING movie and Performance matchups, and the visual archive.
**Test command**: `find client/src/assets -maxdepth 2 -type f \\( -iname '*.png' -o -iname '*.jpg' -o -iname '*.jpeg' -o -iname '*.webp' -o -iname '*.gif' \\) -print0 | sort -z | xargs -0 -r gm identify -format '%f | %wx%h | %m | %b\\n'`
**Sample output**: 29 valid owned WEBP/JPEG files were identified. Concept assets are 1280×1920 or 1920×1280. Poster assets range from 307×480 to 3060×4620 and include the Matrix, Interstellar, Alien, Jaws, Mad Max, Spirited Away, Dark Knight, Godfather, and the poster-backed 1979/Performance pool.
**Processing**: Copy the exact bytes and preserve their current imports, titles, alt text, and poster-availability gating. No remote image hotlinks are introduced.

### Wikidata Query Service
**Used by**: `refreshPixActors`, with roster persistence in `pix_actors` and poster-backed filtering before a Performance matchup can display.
**Test command**: `curl -sS --get 'https://query.wikidata.org/sparql' --data-urlencode 'query=SELECT ?person ?personLabel WHERE { VALUES ?person { wd:Q36949 wd:Q38111 } SERVICE wikibase:label { bd:serviceParam wikibase:language "en". } }' --data-urlencode 'format=json' -H 'Accept: application/sparql-results+json' -H 'User-Agent: MuseArtifactBuilder/1.0'`
**Sample output**: JSON bindings returned Robert De Niro and Leonardo DiCaprio with entity URIs and English labels.
**Processing**: Preserve Brainstorm’s existing bounded refresh action and typed errors. Matchups only display when both stored performers resolve to bundled verified poster artwork; no poster means no matchup.

### Stripe and Backblaze B2
**Used by**: inherited CINE optional cloud quota/checkout and object-storage actions.
**Test evidence**: the inspected `server/src/privileged.ts` reads Stripe at `https://api.stripe.com/v1/checkout/sessions` and uses a server-configured S3-compatible B2 endpoint for signed object operations.
**Processing**: Preserve Brainstorm’s existing secure server-configuration boundary and honest unavailable states. No credentials are copied into source or data; no checkout or upload is initiated during build.

## Image slots
- The 29 existing Brainstorm visual assets remain accepted because the local owned bytes exist and `gm identify` verified a supported JPEG/WEBP format.
- The 12 concept/gallery images preserve their exact existing titles and image-key mappings.
- The original 17 movie posters retain their film/performance mappings and strict “poster 100% of the time or do not display” gate.
- The 1979 discovery deck adds 16 owned poster files, bringing that year from 8 to 24 poster-backed films. Every added image was located through the image-search skill, downloaded into `client/src/assets/pix-posters/`, inspected as JPEG/WEBP, and manually checked against its title before inclusion.
- The runtime-provided lightbulb icon is preserved and is not regenerated.

## Long-term data behavior
- **Refresh policy**: No cron or hook. PIX renders on open. The Wikidata actor roster refresh remains an explicit typed action/manual control; Stripe and B2 are called only from the existing user-driven CINE flows.
- **Growth**: Fresh independent PIX storage grows through user-created notes, dreams, votes, chat messages, CINE metadata, ads, and deployment snapshots. Built-in section/poster foundations seed exactly as in the cloned migrations; no invented user history is copied or created.
- **Ordering**: Preserve Brainstorm’s current ordering — poster sort order, newest-first dreams and notes where implemented, random poster-backed matchups, and current timeline/library ordering.
- **Time semantics**: Automatic creation/update instants remain server timestamps rendered viewer-local; dream dates remain user-entered calendar dates.
- **Independence**: PIX and Brainstorm share code and owned build-time assets at the moment of cloning but have separate databases and future edit histories.

## Rejected approaches
- **Rename or replace Brainstorm**: Rejected because the user explicitly asked for two separate instances.
- **Convert PIX into only the existing movie-choice game**: Rejected because the final clarification asks for a copy of the whole Brainstorm artifact.
- **Copy Brainstorm’s live database, WAL, blobs, audit history, or build output**: Rejected to avoid merging private runtime state or coupling the two instances; the clone initializes independently from migrations.
- **Add new web content or synthetic records**: Rejected because the request is an exact copy, not an expansion.

## 2026-10-13 — Dracula (1931) classic-film showcase

### User-provided storage destination
- Backblaze B2 bucket: `public-domain`
- Object prefix: `pix/dracula-1931/clips/`
- Expected files: `dracula-1931-clip-01.mp4` through `dracula-1931-clip-38.mp4`
- The provider-specific public download origin is not guessed or hard-coded. The `refreshPixClassicStreams` action obtains Backblaze's verified `downloadUrl` from the saved B2 connection, verifies which exact objects exist, then writes one collection-level `stream_base_url` and the corresponding full per-segment `stream_url` values.

### Local source verification
- Source files: `/home/hatch/workspace/pix-network/classic-clips/dracula-1931/clips/dracula-1931-clip-01.mp4` through `dracula-1931-clip-38.mp4`
- `ffprobe` verified all 38 files. Segments 01–37 are approximately 119.035 seconds each; segment 38 is 63.396 seconds. Those measured durations are stored as milliseconds in `pix_classic_clips`.

### Long-term behavior
- `pix_classic_films` stores the one configurable stream base, bucket, and prefix for the film.
- `pix_classic_clips` stores all 38 ordered records: segment number, title, filename, full stream URL, and measured duration.
- The landing-page player reads the database-backed list, lets viewers jump to any segment, and advances to the next segment when playback ends. Before the public objects can be verified, the complete chapter selector remains visible with an honest awaiting-upload state.

**Cycle policy:** A year deck excludes recently shown titles until fewer than two remain, then begins a fresh randomized cycle while avoiding the immediately previous pair where possible.

**Stats:** Movie metadata and aggregate stats (wins, losses, appearances, unseen, per-game appearances, last played) are persisted in `pix_movie_stats`; every vote or unseen answer updates those rows so later game formats can reuse the history.

## 2026 artwork correction pass — 2026-09-30
The full 100-image 2026 contact sheet was reviewed against the catalog title attached to each IMDb identifier. Two mismatches were replaced with owned poster bytes:
- Avengers: Doomsday · tt21357150 · `2026-tt21357150.webp` · page `https://www.tribute.ca/movies/poster/avengers-doomsday-290670?src=new` · image `https://static1.tribute.ca/poster/660x980/avengers-doomsday-290670.jpg`
- Moana · tt27419466 · `2026-tt27419466.webp` · page `https://www.trybooking.com/events/landing/1613697` · image `https://www.trybooking.com/UserData/2026/7/1613697_MOANALIVEACTIONPOSTER_080726010644.jpeg`

Both replacement locators came from the image-search skill, passed cross-origin preflight and image-magic checks, were visually checked against the intended films, and were converted to local WebP files. The shipped client continues to make no remote poster requests.


## DB-driven catalog, 2024 deck, Film DNA, and comparison data — 2026-09-30
- **Catalog storage:** `pix_movie_stats` is the authoritative movie catalog. Titles, years, IMDb IDs, bundled `poster_key` references, source URLs, synopsis/source fields, optional attributes, and accumulated movie stats now live in SQLite. `pix_movie_decks` and `pix_movie_deck_entries` define active year decks and order. No movie catalog, deck membership, poster allowlist, performer-film map, or generated IMDb URL map remains hard-coded in the TypeScript source.
- **Bundled images:** Poster bytes remain owned build-time assets under `client/src/assets/pix-posters/`. The client discovers those files with `import.meta.glob` and resolves only the database-provided `poster_key`; a matchup is withheld unless both poster keys resolve.
- **2024 deck:** 100 verified title/IMDb rows, 100 owned WebP poster files, and 100 synopsis/source records were added from the completed research packet `research_notes/pix-2024-film-deck-20260930-1632`. Exact per-poster source locators remain in that packet’s `notes/posters.json`; synopsis URLs are stored per movie in `pix_movie_stats.synopsis_source_url`. Look Back was re-sourced to the verified Japanese poster at `https://stat.ameba.jp/user_images/20241114/10/andesumashinogogo/55/8a/j/o1080143915509822351.jpg?caw=800` from `https://ameblo.jp/andesumashinogogo/entry-12874964177.html`.
- **Film DNA:** The action searches only poster-backed catalog rows with stored synopses. Inference returns constrained IMDb IDs and match reasoning; it does not invent catalog entries. A bounded `ctx.tool.web_search` pass retrieves current watch/rent/buy result URLs at request time, and the UI labels the search timestamp. Search errors are returned as visible, non-technical states.
- **Reviews:** `pix_movie_review_scores` is the durable home for real Global, Country, and State/Province aggregate scores. No aggregate rows are seeded because no verified review source has been connected. Until real data exists the chart renders personal data only and states that aggregate comparisons are still building.
- **Optional demographics:** `gender`, `age_range`, `race_ethnicity`, `faith`, `country`, and `region` are user-editable optional profile fields. They are used only for game/recommendation context and aggregate comparison scope, matching the in-product privacy promise.
- **Current deck state:** 2026 (100), 2025 (100), 2024 (100), and 1979 (24) are active DB-backed decks. The wider 1895–2023 backfill is not represented as complete.

## 2023 hundred-poster deck — 2026-09-30
**Film metadata:** 100 films first released in 2023 were selected from a 140-result Wikidata Query Service set, ordered by Wikidata sitelink count as a stable popularity proxy. Exact IMDb identifiers and English titles were persisted in `pix_movie_stats`; membership and position were persisted in `pix_movie_deck_entries` under the `year-2023` deck. Source: `https://query.wikidata.org/sparql`.

**Poster acquisition:** Each poster was found through the image-search skill using the exact IMDb identifier plus film title, downloaded as owned JPEG/PNG/WebP bytes, validated with GraphicsMagick, visually checked in four labeled contact sheets, and registered by its database-owned `poster_key`. The client makes zero remote poster requests. Exact retained source locators:

- 2023 · Oppenheimer · tt15398776 · `2023-tt15398776.webp` · page `https://dubaisavers.com/news/movies-of-the-week/` · image `https://cinema.aptrixx.com/posters_original/tt15398776.jpg`

## 2022 deck and 1979 completion — 2026-09-30
**Film identity and poster source:** IMDb suggestion API records captured in `/home/hatch/workspace/research_notes/pix-2022-1979-decks-20260930-1809/` and `/home/hatch/workspace/research_notes/pix-1979-exact-gap-20260930-1901/`. Every imported record is a verified 2022 or 1979 feature with an exact IMDb ID and IMDb primary-poster URL. The three already-owned 1979 posters (Rocky II, The Black Stallion, Quadrophenia) retain their prior files.

**Processing:** Poster bytes are preflighted, downloaded at build time, converted to owned WebP files, and registered in the generated client manifest. Movie rows and deck membership are persisted in `pix_movie_stats`, `pix_movie_decks`, and `pix_movie_deck_entries`; the client contains no movie metadata. Failed poster fetches are not inserted into the deck.

## 2021 year deck (2026-09-30)
- **Film identity and metadata:** 100 distinct 2021 feature films were verified by live web research against IMDb-indexed title identities; each database row stores its exact IMDb title URL, release year, genre label, and stable IMDb ID.
- **Poster artwork:** Each film uses a film-specific poster resolved through the Wikipedia pageimages API. The original image URL and corresponding Wikipedia File page are recorded in the research dataset at `~/workspace/research_notes/pix-2021-deck-20260930-1916/data/films-2021.json`; accepted bytes are copied into `client/src/assets/pix-posters/` and rendered through the generated local asset manifest, never hotlinked.
- **Processing:** `drizzle/0018_add_2021_movie_deck.sql` upserts all 100 film rows, creates the `year-2021` deck, and inserts exactly 100 ordered deck entries. Existing movie stats are preserved on conflict.
- **Source limitation:** Wikipedia's fair-use poster originals are intentionally modest resolution; no generated stand-ins or unrelated imagery were substituted.

## 2020 hundred-poster deck — 2026-09-30
**Film metadata:** Exactly 100 distinct 2020 feature films were researched and verified by IMDb identifier. Research report: `~/workspace/research_notes/pix-2020-film-deck-20260930-2030/report.md`.

**Poster acquisition:** Each poster was found through the approved image-search skill with title, year, and IMDb ID, then downloaded into owned build-time assets, validated as a supported image, converted to WebP, and visually reviewed in a labeled contact sheet. The app renders owned bytes only; remote locators below are provenance.

## 2019 hundred-poster deck — 2026-09-30
**Film metadata:** Exactly 100 distinct 2019 feature films were researched and verified by IMDb identifier. Research report: `~/workspace/research_notes/pix-2019-film-deck-20260930-2051/report.md`.

**Poster acquisition:** Each poster was found through the approved image-search skill using title, year, and IMDb ID, then downloaded into owned build-time assets, validated as a supported image, converted to WebP, and visually reviewed in four 25-poster contact sheets. The app renders owned bytes only; remote locators below are provenance.

## 2018 hundred-poster deck — 2026-09-30
**Film selection and identity:** Candidate titles were supplied by the completed 2018 research report. Exact 2018 IMDb identifiers were checked through IMDb Suggest; Girl, Whitney, McQueen, Quincy, and Far from the Tree were additionally verified by search because automated lookup was incomplete. Metadata, stable IDs, poster keys, source locators, and stats are persisted in the database; the client manifest contains asset imports only.

**Poster acquisition:** Every poster below was found by the image-search workflow or the IMDb Suggest image result, downloaded at build time into owned artifact assets, verified as an image, and converted to WebP. The client makes no remote poster requests.

**Refresh policy:** This is a fixed historical deck; no runtime refresh is needed. New votes and unseen marks continue accumulating against the stable movie ID.

## Top 100 Horror canon (1950–2026) — added 2026-09-30

- **Core rows:** 100 distinct feature-length horror films selected as a broad critical canon synthesized from Time Out, Rotten Tomatoes editorial, IndieWire, Variety, The Guardian, BFI/Sight and Sound, and Entertainment Weekly. Exact rank/title/year/IMDb identity and per-row editorial URLs came from the research report at `research_notes/top-100-horror-films-1950-2026-20260930-2348/report.md`.
- **Placement:** Every film is stored in `pix_movie_stats` and inserted into its release-year `pix_movie_deck_entries` deck. Existing deck rows and statistics are preserved; missing year deck shells are created without fabricated filler.
- **Poster acquisition:** 92 new poster images were sourced by exact title + release year + IMDb ID through the image-search skill, preflighted as browser-safe raster images, converted to bundled WebP assets, and visually reviewed in contact sheets. Eight films reuse already bundled, exact-IMDb-ID poster assets. All poster keys remain database-owned; the generated client manifest is only an asset import map.
- **Refresh policy:** fixed historical canon; no runtime refresh. PIX votes, unseen marks, and FILM DNA signals continue accumulating against stable IMDb IDs.

## Earliest-year honest-count deck — 2026-10-01
**Used by**: the selectable 1895 Movie vs Movie year deck and the year selector’s explicit real-poster-backed count.

**Film identity sources**:
- Workers Leaving the Lumière Factory — `https://www.imdb.com/fr/title/tt0000010/`
- L'Arroseur Arrosé — `https://www.imdb.com/title/tt0000014`
- Annabelle Serpentine Dance — `https://www.imdb.com/title/tt0154152`

**Poster source pages and exact image locators**:
- Workers Leaving the Lumière Factory — `https://a.ltrbxd.com/resized/film-poster/5/1/3/7/8/51378-workers-leaving-the-lumiere-factory-0-1000-0-1500-crop.jpg?v=839b056be1`
- L'Arroseur Arrosé — source context `https://www.bridgemanimages.com/en/auzolle/poster-for-the-movie-l-arroseur-arrose-1895-poster/lithograph/asset/164131`; accepted unwatermarked poster locator `https://xl.movieposterdb.com/09_12/1895/14/xl_14_97acaa84.jpg`
- Annabelle Serpentine Dance — source page `https://darklyst.com/movie/detail/danse-serpentine-annabelle-/144410`; poster locator `https://image.tmdb.org/t/p/w342/nn5Syx6Cy5QF5CsQv0oEqeA5Je5.jpg`

**Processing**: Every accepted image returned HTTP 200 with JPEG file magic, was inspected visually, converted to owned WebP under `client/src/assets/pix-posters/`, and registered by stable `<year>-<imdb-id>.webp` key. The deck contains three genuine individually titled poster artworks; no production still, historical lithograph, or invented title was accepted. Its database target is three, and year controls say “3 real poster-backed films” rather than implying 100.

## Halloween horror waves and FILM DNA taxonomy — 2026-10-01
**Used by**: database-backed horror catalog expansion and FILM DNA matching.

**Data basis**: The existing poster-backed year-deck rows and owned poster bytes established in migrations 0013 and 0018–0023 and documented above. No remote poster URLs are rendered at runtime and no new synthetic movie records are created for these waves.

**Processing**:
- Wave 1 records the original 100-film horror canon already added in migration 0023.
- Wave 2 classifies 54 additional owned, poster-backed horror films from 1979 and the 2018–2021 year decks.
- Wave 3 classifies 40 additional owned, poster-backed horror films from the 2024–2026 year decks.
- All 194 classified films remain members of their release-year decks. The normalized `pix_horror_tags` and `pix_movie_horror_tags` tables store many-to-many labels and wave membership by stable IMDb ID; application source contains no movie list.
- The taxonomy includes Horror, Goriest, Scariest, Best Monster, Scariest Monsters, Best Halloween, Alien, Slasher, Paranormal, Body Horror, Comedy Horror, Creature Feature, Folk Horror, Found Footage, Gothic, Home Invasion, Occult, Psychological, Sci-Fi Horror, Supernatural, Survival, Vampire, Werewolf, Witchcraft, and Zombie. Superlative labels are explicitly editorial collections rather than objective rankings.
- FILM DNA synopsis matching receives these stored labels as evidence, and matchup votes fold the winner and loser’s stored horror labels into the same consent-gated preference weights used for genre learning.

**Long-term behavior**: the deck list, exact 100-film order, metadata, poster references, and cumulative per-film stats are database-owned. Existing wins, losses, appearances, unseen counts, and game-cycle counters survive metadata upserts. The client contains only the build-time asset-key manifest, not movie rows.

**Image processing**: each poster candidate came from the image-search skill, passed a browser-header fetch and image-magic preflight, was visually checked in four 25-poster contact sheets, converted to owned WEBP bytes under `client/src/assets/pix-posters/`, and is imported by the generated manifest.

**Long-term behavior**: the deck list, exact 100-film order, metadata, poster references, and cumulative per-film stats are database-owned. Existing wins, losses, appearances, unseen counts, and game-cycle counters survive metadata upserts. The client contains only the build-time asset-key manifest, not movie rows.

## Year-deck continuation: 2003–2002 — 2026-09-30

- **Film identity and ranking:** IMDb public datasets (`title.basics.tsv.gz` and `title.ratings.tsv.gz`) supplied exact IMDb IDs, release years, title types, genres, ratings, and vote counts. For each year, the selection walks highest vote count first among films and keeps the first 100 rows for which a real poster was obtained. IMDb title links are stored per movie in `pix_movie_stats`.
- **Posters:** Every retained movie was queried by exact title, year, and IMDb ID with the image-search skill. Each image locator was preflighted with a browser-style cross-origin request, required to decode as JPEG/PNG/GIF/WebP, required to be portrait-oriented and at least 350 pixels tall, converted to owned WebP bytes, and mapped by a database-owned poster key. The client contains only the generated key-to-asset import map; titles, years, genres, sources, deck membership, and ordering live in the database.
- **Deck floor:** Each year deck is populated without a 100-row cap. Existing poster-backed films from horror/thriller waves remain in their release year, so a deck may exceed 100.
- **Refresh:** Historical snapshot; no runtime refresh. User vote/unseen statistics continue accumulating against stable IMDb IDs.

## Year-deck continuation: 1995–1995 — 2026-09-30

- **Film identity and ranking:** IMDb public datasets (`title.basics.tsv.gz` and `title.ratings.tsv.gz`) supplied exact IMDb IDs, release years, title types, genres, ratings, and vote counts. For each year, the selection walks highest vote count first among films and keeps the first 100 rows for which a real poster was obtained. IMDb title links are stored per movie in `pix_movie_stats`.
- **Posters:** Every retained movie was queried by exact title, year, and IMDb ID with the image-search skill. Each image locator was preflighted with a browser-style cross-origin request, required to decode as JPEG/PNG/GIF/WebP, required to be portrait-oriented and at least 350 pixels tall, converted to owned WebP bytes, and mapped by a database-owned poster key. The client contains only the generated key-to-asset import map; titles, years, genres, sources, deck membership, and ordering live in the database.
- **Deck floor:** Each year deck is populated without a 100-row cap. Existing poster-backed films from horror/thriller waves remain in their release year, so a deck may exceed 100.
- **Refresh:** Historical snapshot; no runtime refresh. User vote/unseen statistics continue accumulating against stable IMDb IDs.

## Year-deck continuation: 1999–1999 — 2026-09-30

- **Film identity and ranking:** IMDb public datasets (`title.basics.tsv.gz` and `title.ratings.tsv.gz`) supplied exact IMDb IDs, release years, title types, genres, ratings, and vote counts. For each year, the selection walks highest vote count first among films and keeps the first 100 rows for which a real poster was obtained. IMDb title links are stored per movie in `pix_movie_stats`.
- **Posters:** Every retained movie was queried by exact title, year, and IMDb ID with the image-search skill. Each image locator was preflighted with a browser-style cross-origin request, required to decode as JPEG/PNG/GIF/WebP, required to be portrait-oriented and at least 350 pixels tall, converted to owned WebP bytes, and mapped by a database-owned poster key. The client contains only the generated key-to-asset import map; titles, years, genres, sources, deck membership, and ordering live in the database.
- **Deck floor:** Each year deck is populated without a 100-row cap. Existing poster-backed films from horror/thriller waves remain in their release year, so a deck may exceed 100.
- **Refresh:** Historical snapshot; no runtime refresh. User vote/unseen statistics continue accumulating against stable IMDb IDs.

## Complete 1895–2026 year-deck catalog — finalized 2026-10-01

- **Structured source:** IMDb public datasets (`title.basics.tsv.gz` and `title.ratings.tsv.gz`) supplied stable IMDb IDs, release years, title types, genres, ratings, and vote counts. Every movie row stores its exact IMDb title URL in `pix_movie_stats.source_url`.
- **Artwork source and ownership:** Posters were found by exact title/year/IMDb-ID image search, preflighted as decodable portrait JPEG/PNG/GIF/WebP media at least 350 px tall, and copied into `client/src/assets/pix-posters/`. Every retained row stores the exact poster byte URL in `pix_movie_stats.poster_source_url`; result-page provenance is retained in `attributes_json.posterSourcePage` when returned. The generated `pixPosterManifest.ts` only maps database poster keys to owned build-time assets and contains no movie metadata.
- **Completion:** Every year from 1915 through 2026 has at least 100 poster-backed films. Existing valid extras were preserved, including 2019 (101), 2014 (144), 2013 (105), 2012 (105), 2011 (104), 2010 (103), 2009 (105), 2008 (104), 2007 (104), 2006 (105), 2005 (104), 2003 (101), 2001 (102), and 1979 (135). One hundred is a floor, never a cap.
- **Sparse early cinema:** 1895–1914 use honest lower targets because enough distinct title-matched poster artwork was not available. Final active counts: 1895: 3; 1896: 9; 1897: 3; 1898: 2; 1900: 7; 1901: 3; 1903: 2; 1904: 4; 1905: 6; 1906: 5; 1907: 8; 1908: 11; 1909: 7; 1910: 8; 1911: 14; 1912: 15; 1913: 24; 1914: 42. The 1899 and 1902 singleton decks were deactivated because a head-to-head cannot run.
- **Integrity pass:** For 1896–1914, retained artwork required title-matched provenance plus the release year or exact IMDb ID in the source text. Entries backed by stills, unrelated films, generic vendor art, or ambiguous search results were removed from deck membership and made ineligible for all poster games by clearing their poster key.
- **Identity correction:** `Locke` (`tt2692904`) now belongs to the 2013 deck, matching IMDb's release year, and was removed from 2014.
- **Refresh:** Historical database snapshot. Matchup votes, unseen marks, and game appearances continue accumulating under stable IMDb IDs.

## Round-two year-deck expansion — 2026 and 2025 (2026-10-09)

- **Film identity and ranking:** The existing locally owned IMDb public dataset snapshots (`title.basics.tsv.gz` and `title.ratings.tsv.gz`) supplied stable IMDb IDs, release years, movie title type, genres, source ratings, and vote counts. Existing `pix_movie_stats` IDs were excluded before ranking. For each year, candidates were ordered by source vote count, then source rating, then title; the first 100 candidates whose poster searches yielded a preflighted real poster were selected.
- **Poster discovery:** `/opt/hatch/bin/image-search` was queried with the exact title, release year, IMDb ID, and “original theatrical movie poster.” Each stored byte URL and source page below is copied verbatim from that result.
- **Poster acceptance:** each byte URL passed a browser-style HEAD request and byte download, decoded as JPEG/PNG/GIF/WebP, had portrait aspect ratio at least 1.18 h/w, and was at least 350px tall. Accepted bytes were converted to owned WebP assets no larger than 700×1050 and packed into the matching year archive. The game never uses a row without a poster.
- **Database behavior:** migration `0039_second_catalog_expansion_2026_2025.sql` appends these rows to the existing year decks without deleting or replacing prior films, raises both deck targets from 100 to 200, and stores all title, year, genre, poster-key, source, and selection metadata in SQLite. The TypeScript manifest maps only poster asset keys to owned archives; it contains no film metadata.
- **Preflight command:** `curl -fsSLI -A "Mozilla/5.0" -H "Accept: image/png,image/jpeg,image/gif,image/webp,image/*;q=0.5" -H "Referer: https://www.example.org/" <byte-url> && curl -fsSL ... -o <temp> <byte-url> && gm identify -format "%w %h %m" <temp>`
- **Verification query:** `SELECT d.release_year, COUNT(*) entries, COUNT(DISTINCT e.imdb_id) distinct_films, COUNT(DISTINCT s.poster_key) posters FROM pix_movie_decks d JOIN pix_movie_deck_entries e USING(deck_key) JOIN pix_movie_stats s USING(imdb_id) WHERE d.release_year IN (2026,2025) GROUP BY d.release_year ORDER BY d.release_year DESC;`
- **Expected output:** `2026 | 200 | 200 | 200` and `2025 | 200 | 200 | 200`.

## Uncapped catalog expansion — 2023 complete, 2022 started — 2026-10-11
- **No per-year ceiling:** `pix_movie_decks.target_size` remains a progress milestone, not an insertion cap. The 2023 milestone is now 200 and the 2022 milestone is 111; future verified additions can continue beyond either value.
- **2023 round two:** 100 distinct 2023 IMDb IDs were recovered from the already-owned `2023.zip` poster archive and excluded against all 100 existing 2023 deck IDs. Exact title, movie type, release year, genres, rating, and vote count came from the existing local IMDb public `title.basics.tsv.gz` and `title.ratings.tsv.gz` snapshots. All 100 poster files were identified as supported images and visually reviewed in four 25-poster contact sheets; they depict posters rather than stills. The migration stores every movie and deck membership in SQLite, positions 101–200; TypeScript contains no movie rows.
- **2022 continuation:** The next 11 highest-vote distinct 2022 IMDb movie rows for which a real portrait poster passed the image-search, cross-origin fetch, image-magic, dimension, and visual-review checks were added as positions 101–111. One questionable inset image was rejected and not shipped. Exact retained source locators:
  - The Kashmir Files · `tt10811166` · poster `https://xl.movieposterdb.com/22_03/2020/10811166/xl_10811166_13b5d7d4.jpg` · page `https://it-id.movieposterdb.com/the-kashmir-files-i10811166/13b5d7d4`
  - Talk to Me · `tt10638522` · poster `https://xl.movieposterdb.com/23_10/2023/10638522/xl_talk-to-me-movie-poster_288c9bbc.jpg` · page `https://sheares.link/talk-to-me-2023-tt10638522`
  - Death on the Nile · `tt7657566` · poster `https://images.theposterdb.com/prod/public/images/posters/optimized/movies/51393/HiEFKLVH25TCBxvK8iwCIMRnCkn7moyP5QtP4mLH.webp`
  - M3GAN · `tt8760708` · poster `https://imgix.hoyts.com.au/mx/posters/au/m3gan-b906a8d7.jpg`
  - Laal Singh Chaddha · `tt10028196` · poster `https://flxt.tmsimg.com/assets/p20824854_p_v13_aa.jpg`
  - Morbius · `tt5108870` · poster `https://aragonoutlook.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/04/Morbius-poster-1-405x600.png` · page `https://aragonoutlook.org/page/66/`
  - K.G.F: Chapter 2 · `tt10698680` · poster `https://images.justwatch.com/poster/249962156/s592/k-g-f-chapter-2.%7Bformat%7D`

- **Owned media:** The 11 accepted 2022 poster bytes are stored in `client/src/assets/pix-poster-archives/2022.zip` and registered in the existing archive manifest. No remote poster is rendered at runtime.
- **Progress:** 2023 is complete at 200 verified poster-backed films. 2022 is at 111 and is the active backward-expansion year. No decade completion is claimed by this batch.


## Live trending feeds — 2026-10-11

- **Core movie-trending source:** TMDB API v3, fetched only from server action `refreshPixTrending` and cached in `pix_trending` with source rank and pull time. Runtime host: `api.themoviedb.org`.
- **Exact runtime endpoints:**
  - `https://api.themoviedb.org/3/trending/movie/day?language=en-US`
  - `https://api.themoviedb.org/3/trending/movie/week?language=en-US`
  - `https://api.themoviedb.org/3/movie/now_playing?language=en-US&page=1&region=US`
- **Credential:** the verified PIX owner pastes the TMDB API Read Access Token once in Settings. The value is stored only in the server-side `pix_config.secret_value` column, is never returned by any action, and is sent only as a server-side `Authorization` header to TMDB. Settings also accepts YouTube Data, Pixabay, Freesound, and Vimeo credentials for later server-proxied lookups; each remains marked only `configured` until a real server request verifies the connection.
- **Storage / matching:** only TMDB titles that match a real, poster-backed `pix_movie_stats` row by normalized title and release year are stored. Unmatched titles are omitted rather than synthesized. The three source snapshots are replaced together after a successful non-empty match; source errors and missing configuration leave the last good snapshot intact and write an honest run status to `pix_trending_runs`.
- **UI provenance:** each movie exposes its contributing TMDB source(s) plus the stored pull date. No-key and no-snapshot states remain visibly empty.
- **CINE shorts source:** first-party `cine_assets` rows on PIX.MOV, ranked from `cine_short_stats` (`views`, `plays`, `completions`, and a derived completion rate). Signals are recorded only from actual card visibility and video playback. No external source and no invented starter metrics.


## YouTube trending short-form ingestion (2026-10-11)

- **Source:** YouTube Data API v3 `videos.list`, `chart=mostPopular`, US region.
- **Source documentation:** https://developers.google.com/youtube/v3/docs/videos
- **Refresh shape:** The server action requests `snippet` and `contentDetails`, filters the returned chart to ISO 8601 durations of 180 seconds or less, preserves chart order as a short-form rank, and stores a dated snapshot in `pix_trending` with source `youtube_shorts`.
- **UI use:** The Shorts rail reads only the stored snapshot. Playback uses YouTube's official embed endpoint with a visible YouTube link-out fallback. No YouTube CDN thumbnail is hotlinked or persisted.
- **Credential handling:** The configured API key remains in `pix_config`; requests send it server-side in the `X-Goog-Api-Key` header and never return it to the client.


## Backblaze B2 Classic Clips storage (2026-10-11)

- **Source:** User-configured Backblaze B2 bucket via the B2 Native API.
- **Source documentation:** https://www.backblaze.com/apidocs/b2-authorize-account-2
- **Credential handling:** B2 key ID, application key, and bucket name are submitted to a server action, stored together only in the write-only `pix_config` secret field, and never returned to the client. Authorization tokens remain server-side and are sent only in Backblaze request headers.
- **Listing:** `listPixB2Videos` authenticates, resolves the configured bucket, lists up to 1,000 current file names, and returns only video metadata (file ID/name, MIME type, size, upload time).
- **Playback preparation:** `streamPixB2Video` verifies a selected file through B2, downloads it server-to-server with the authorization header, enforces a 250 MB ceiling, copies the bytes into artifact-owned blob storage, and returns only the owned blob URL for native PIX playback. The future Classic Clips UI can call these actions without exposing B2 credentials or hotlinking expiring B2 media URLs.

## PIX Shorts sequence policy — 2026-10-12
**User-defined rule:** Native PIX.MOV Shorts are limited to 120 seconds. At the end of each clip (or at the 120-second hard stop), the player shows a five-second full-page ad break and then advances to the next clip in sequence.

**Data handling:** CINE clip uploads now persist a measured integer `duration_seconds`; the server rejects uploads longer than 120 seconds and the Shorts feed excludes legacy assets without a verified duration. Stored YouTube discovery rows are filtered to 120 seconds or less at both refresh and read time. Classic Dracula clips retain their filename-derived order and use the existing owned PIX blob streaming path.

**Advertising:** The existing managed ad model adds a `shorts` placement. An active Shorts-placement ad supplies the full-page break content; when none is configured, the same timed slot renders a neutral PIX.MOV house slate and still advances after five seconds.

## 2026-10-20 — Verified-live public-domain classics wired (INGEST §8)

**User direction**: Wire the 31 verified-live public-domain films (INGEST template §8) into PIX.MOV: SCREENING page (FEATURE PRESENTATION) with player + clip list that streams with no waiting state, a CINE PIX LIBRARY entry with working ADD to BIN, and landing-channel placement (Comedy for the silent comedies, Horror for the horror films, plus Recently Uploaded). Little Shop of Horrors' stream URL was fixed from a dead link to the correct B2 address.

**Ground truth**: `~/workspace/pix-network/classic-clips/pd100_status.json` (31 films at status `verified-live` with clip counts and B2 prefixes) and each film's `manifest.json` in the same tree (per-clip filename, duration, and full `b2_url`), cross-checked against `ingest-verify-latest.json`. All 31 films live in the Backblaze B2 bucket `PIXMOV` under `public-domain/<genre>/<year>/<filmKey>/clips/` and stream from `https://s3.us-east-005.backblazeb2.com/PIXMOV/...`. First and last clip URLs of all 31 films were re-verified with live HTTP 200 checks during this build. The previously wired base (`https://f002.backblazeb2.com/file/public-domain/pix/<filmKey>/clips/`) returns 404 — that dead pattern is what broke Little Shop of Horrors and would have kept every film in the waiting state.

**New in this wiring**: 14 silent comedies (The General, The Gold Rush, Sherlock Jr., Safety Last!, The Kid, The Circus, The Freshman, Speedy, Steamboat Bill, Jr., The Navigator, The Cameraman, Our Hospitality, The Kid Brother, Big Business), 2 dramas (The Birth of a Nation, Intolerance), and the 2 sci-fi titles (The Brain That Wouldn't Die, Teenagers from Outer Space) alongside the 13 horror films. Plan 9 from Outer Space stays parked: no B2 path in its definition, no stream URLs, unpublished per the standing rights hold.

**Processing**: Film definitions in `server/src/actions.ts` (`PIX_CLASSIC_FILMS`) now carry each film's real `b2Path`; `ensurePixClassicCollection` writes bucket `PIXMOV`, the real prefix, and each clip's public stream URL directly, so `listPixClassicFilms` / `listPixClassicFilm` / `listPixClassicSections` serve ready-to-play collections with no credential-gated refresh. Migration `0061_wire_verified_live_classic_films.sql` performs the same wiring in the database: it repoints the 15 previously wired films and registers the 16 new films with their real per-clip durations from the manifests. Landing placement flows from genre (catalog match first, `PIX_CLASSIC_GENRE_FALLBACKS` second): comedies → Comedy channel, horror → Horror channel, and the new batch leads Recently Uploaded via `PIX_RECENT_UPLOAD_PIN`. CINE's PIX LIBRARY reads the same collections, so ADD to BIN (which requires a clip stream URL) works for every wired clip.

**Poster gap closed during wiring**: The General (1926) had no catalog row and no poster asset anywhere in PIX, which would have silently dropped it from the poster-backed channels. Its real theatrical poster (Buster Keaton billing version, TMDB image CDN: `https://image.tmdb.org/t/p/w1280//bVABKsniQzkqXa2MjcjUGSFsTL9.jpg`, found via image search, page https://jiotv.pro/movies/The-General) was downloaded, verified as the genuine film poster, resized to the archive standards (400×600 full / 60×90 marquee WebP), and added to `client/src/assets/pix-poster-archives/1926.zip` and `client/src/assets/pix-marquee-poster-archives/1926.zip` as `1926-tt0017925.webp` with a matching `pixPosterManifest.ts` entry; migration `0062_add_the_general_1926_catalog_poster.sql` adds its `pix_movie_stats` row. Häxan's definition title was also corrected to the catalog's "Häxan" so its existing poster (1922-tt0013257.webp) resolves and it takes its Horror channel slot.
